| 7 | **Quantum fuzzing of the kernel** | Kernel state snapshot fixed classically after boot; a step budget; syscall arguments as the superposed input over a declared small space; devices virtualised; the oracle bit is the panic flag. The contract is the optimised-IR reference semantics of §3.2 under a recorded definedness assumption; the kernel's own source UB is not detected after optimisation and is not claimed to be. (a) Oracle verified by §5.1 part 1 against native execution of the same snapshot per basis input, with classifier-flagged inputs reported, fault rate zero on the declared space. (b) Amplitude amplification by state-vector simulation at a toy argument space. (c) A resource estimate under a named target. The oracle is Linux. |
| 8 | **Amplitude estimation on a Monte Carlo in plain C** | Written by someone who has never heard of a Toffoli gate. The entropy device is replaced by a superposed register; everything else is fixed classically before entry. (a) Oracle by §5.1 part 1; (b) estimation by simulation at small precision; (c) resource estimate. |

Rungs 1–3 and 5–6 are the blueprint's; 4, 7, 8 define success for the
vision; nobody promises 6–8 on a schedule.

## 9. Open technical questions (deferred by the maintainer's rule: vision first)

In the order the next iteration should take them. Items the council
promoted from "detail" to "load-bearing" are now answered at vision level
above and appear here only for their mechanics.

1. **The legality spike** (§3.2): the marker corpus against every stock
   pass at O3 and through native codegen; emission examples from clang,
   rustc and Julia; gate on freezing the dialect spec.
2. **Handle → wire conversion**: the verifier, what it rejects (including
   the uncomputation-validity invariant and the copy/store/in-place rules
   of §4.2), the leak check.
3. **Compilation model mechanics** (§3.3): LTO extension point vs `opt`;
   fat-object layout; the launch ABI; host-triple intrinsic and asm
   classification tables; bitcode builds of language runtimes.
4. **Budget mechanics**: out-of-band budget tables; scalar-evolution
   refinement; how the specialiser recovers structure from the step
   machine; the exact fault-code set.
5. **Effect virtualisation**: which device models exist (VFS, console,
   clock, entropy, block device, network as a recorded peer), how they are
   expressed as reversible state, what is refused.
6. **Measurement-based uncomputation under control**: the v1
   `PermClean`-style certificate (inherited from the sunset Sturm.jl)
   cannot express it; the certificate kind with classical fixup and its
   controlled form, fixed in Bennett.jl's language before any gate record
   freezes.
7. **Reversible ABI and linking**: call/uncall convention, shared ancilla
   frames, structural symbol resolution, content-addressed objects.
8. **Run-time scheduling policy**: history layers, checkpoint interval,
   delta quality, recomputation of coherent regions, as resource policy
   under the vector of §4.3.
9. **ISA serialisation and versioning**; conformance suite; the formal
   model's scope.
10. **Per-LLVM-major builds**; Julia in-process invocation through LLVM.jl.
11. Concurrency (sequentialised with a recorded schedule; the single-
    thread assumption of §3.2 is the v1 contract), self-modifying code,
    signals, setjmp. Floating point is decided in §3.2 (deterministic
    soft-float in both builds) and is no longer open.
12. Repository hosting and release mechanics for §6.1.

## 10. Process

Vision first (this document), iterate with the council, then design, then
spikes, then code. The current Bennett.jl and BennettVM.jl are frozen as
the v1 reference and differential oracle. No reboot code is written on the
strength of this document. Worklog, beads, red-green, exhaustive
verification, no remote CI carry over unchanged.


# Background (not graded): `fat-object-scope.md`

# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 2 snapshot)

# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

Read the [round-1 report](../../../../bennett/docs/council/pass2-v2v-2026-11-02/round-1/report.md)
and both `out-sol.json` and `out-codex.json` in that directory. The table
maps every blocking item to this revision; it claims a proposed resolution,
not a new council verdict.

| Item | Change and witness |
|---|---|
| sol V2V-1; codex V2V-1 — frames lack a device-root boundary | Append the §12.3 rule below. Witness: device pair's root key is []; flip contexts belong to pair's tree; native main needs no descriptor. Separate root B has its own []. |

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. Every supplied frame_contexts array is validated, including a single-context
array. Coverage includes predicated-off and default-covered realised calls;
frame selection never inspects the quantum predicate value. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A uniform descriptor must also have valid allocation order, scheme identities
and live ranges in every covered invocation. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Fat-object applicability

Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
Append the following proposed normative paragraph to §12.3:

```text
In a mixed fat object, frame and frame_contexts describe the linked
ISA regions in the certificate's bound device-root realisation. Each
device root has its own object/root bindings and complete context set;
[] denotes that device root, never the native executable entry. Native
host functions, process argv/envp and host-only libc need no frame
records. Shared linked closures retain separate root-relative contexts.
A host may reach the device through §10's device CC entry; the general
mixed-container launch shim remains the explicit boundary work described
in the shared scope statement. Native comparison binds the corresponding
materialised device region and callee closure under its recorded configuration.
```

The shared statement lists today's assumptions and the exact remaining
container, launch, budget, handle, tape and host-memory contract changes.
This note does not assume that the device object is the entire binary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Concrete context witness

With H_pair denoting the pair closure hash, the two context keys are
`[{symbol:H_pair,point:"entry:0",iteration:[],instance:[],call_index:0}]`
and the same key with point `entry:1`. Their complete frame descriptors
are `{slots:[{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",class:"A",width:1}],scratch:[],history:[],borrow:[],ancillae:[]}`
and the identical descriptor with class B. Replace H_pair by the bound hash
and sort by unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. These five
arrays are all required B5 arrays. An empty schedule entries array does
not omit either default-covered call or its frame. A separate device
root's [] is independent of pair's [].

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked by [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py). The originating note reports the sibling test's `Unsupported` expectation
as gap evidence. This revision executes no sibling test and claims no
completed consumer fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

## Publication and validation boundary

This is a revision proposal, not council acceptance or a consumer implementation.
The default publication rule in spec/README.md requires a full version bump
for semantic changes. The recommended 0.9 K route requires explicit council
and maintainer authorization of its in-version compatibility rules; this note
does not grant it. All unchanged-hash claims assume unchanged header/source
and catalog envelopes. A full version publication recomputes affected symbol,
object, target, schedule, certificate and versioned ref-library identities and
consumer pins even when recipe bodies stay fixed. Published 0.9 bytes remain
available; older closed-schema readers require an upgrade for new fields.

The reproducible finite and document checks are in [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py):
`timeout 120 python3 -I -B /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2/check_rev2.py`.
Executed for this revision: exit 0, all eight PASS result lines.
These finite checks verify witnesses and revision structure; they implement
no proposed-format checker or host shim. No full repository gate or sibling gate is claimed: scripts/pre-push
uses git/worktree writes, forbidden by this runner, and its setup changes paths
outside this job. Underlying beads remain open for publication and consumers.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Contract pass 2 — council decision index and verification

These are proposals and dispositions, not spec edits or implementation
acceptance. All 13 requested beads were read with `bd show`. ISA records
were read in their originating checkout. Dialect `bd show` could not open
the sibling's read-only Dolt LOCK, including with `--readonly`; copying its
live embedded database to job scratch allowed both `bd --readonly show`
commands to succeed without writing the source repository. The passive
JSONL export was not imported or used as the authoritative database.

The binding COMMON/CLAUDE rules, all first-pass contract notes and the
historical K82 note were read before derivation. Citations below refer to
ISA `spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md`, both maintained dialect specifications
under `../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/`, North Star
`../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md`, and the council's ISA rubric.
Originating requests, source fixtures and selected implementation boundaries
were inspected read-only as each note identifies.

| Bead | Verdict | Recommended route |
|---|---|---|
| [b-isa-spec-0jq](b-isa-spec-0jq.md) | Structural-only applicability and action reference hash are gaps; qinplace owner already specified | 0.9 K; null/no-census semantics and linked owner-record checks |
| [b-isa-spec-4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) | One legal mixed-class slot has no B5 descriptor | 0.9 K/B5 class fragments; preserve slot identity |
| [b-isa-spec-v2v](b-isa-spec-v2v.md) | One closure's frames can differ across equal variant/control contexts | 0.9 K; exact context-indexed frames with uniform shorthand |
| [b-isa-spec-7s0](b-isa-spec-7s0.md) | Existing restricted D_F proof has no frame/workspace claim binding | 0.9 K; selected-frame tensor relation, exact K76/K78 entry domain |
| [b-isa-spec-95w](b-isa-spec-95w.md) | Already covered native-model gap; add exact-entry/context/digest qualifications | First-pass 0.9 K plus versioned model; if 0.10, retain explicit current blocker |

No capability in the North Star thesis, §§3–5 or §8 ladder is removed.
In particular all unmodified SQLite, Julia, operating-system, Grover,
kernel-fuzzing and Monte Carlo requirements remain. An unchecked report
or a mechanics deferral records remaining work; it does not complete or
reduce that scope. No original issue is closed: council decisions and
consumer implementations remain with the listed beads. These findings
are already tracked; no duplicate or cross-repo tracker mutation is needed.

## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

## Commands and actual results

env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY -u DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS -u BROWSER \
  TMPDIR=/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/.tmp/sol-contract-pass2/tmp \

The environment above is a command prefix. Scratch and the required
single Resume block are under `.tmp/sol-contract-pass2/`; durable
deliverables are only this index and the 13 sibling notes.

The script additionally checks an in-memory body-order correction, writes
only its resulting fixture to job scratch, and compares both documents
after removing interface-enumeration differences (31 interfaces and 10
edge-interface arrays). Every other field,
including all 86 leaf digests and 16 cases, is identical. It independently
counts the 31 differing interfaces. The corrected fixture probe passes;
this does not edit or claim acceptance of the published fixture repair.
An initial combined run timed out at a 45-second per-probe debug limit
after the document/arithmetic/CLI checks passed. The final command uses
120 seconds per probe and 300 seconds overall; no assertion was removed.
Final document/arithmetic additions were rechecked with the same block's
prefix through the line before `def run(argv):`; the unchanged reference
and probe checks above were already complete.
The catalog/document-construction portion through the line before
`for path in (original_path, corrected_path):` was also re-executed:
31 differing interfaces, 10 differing edge arrays, and both artifact
digests below passed. These final checks used `timeout 30 python3 -I -B
-u -`, with the same extraction and `compile`/`exec` method and a distinct
`validation_source` variable so the executed document code could not
overwrite the extraction buffer.

## Reproducible finite checks

From the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
timeout 300 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2/INDEX.md')
s = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(s, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

This code checks exact small mathematical witnesses and actual published
reference interfaces. The arithmetic is not an implementation of any new
certificate verifier or a substitute for its required conformance tests.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2'
SCRATCH = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-contract-pass2'
REF = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
PROBE = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/k82-probe'
assert Path.cwd() == ROOT
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]
def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
params = hashlib.sha256(jcs(args)).hexdigest()
assert params == hashlib.sha256(jcs(copy.deepcopy(args))).hexdigest()
assert jcs({'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,0]}) != jcs(
           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})
assert (4 if partition([20,20]) == [0,0] else 3) == 4
assert (4 if partition([20,30]) == [0,0] else 3) == 3

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)
# djm: required public name/environment examples, not a musl process simulation.
assert '/bin/demo'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == 'demo'
assert '/bin/'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == ''
assert dict(s.split('=',1) for s in ['K=V'])['K'] == 'V'
print('PASS: structural, frame/resource, borrow, census, token, allocation and process witnesses')

def run(argv):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=ROOT, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout
fixture = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/controlled-action-09/controlled.bisa'
pin = '45bbb506541e3a8bb28a18e1bb0aef16ba01ee1e7cac74d5a190deb3ad36ef5a'
for command in ('check', 'hash'):
    result = json.loads(run([REF, command, fixture]))
    assert result['status'] == 'ok' and result['object_hash'] == pin
for t, c in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    for initial, adjoint, expected in [(t, False, t ^ c), (t ^ c, True, t)]:
        cmd = [REF, 'run-action', fixture, 'controlled', '--mut', str(initial), '--in', str(c)]
        if adjoint:
            cmd += ['--adjoint']
        result = json.loads(run(cmd))
        assert result['mut'] == [expected] and result['in'] == [c]
        assert result['state'] == {str(expected)+str(c): [1,0]}
print('PASS: published B5/K13 check/hash identity and 4 forward/4 adjoint action cases')

# ojy: independent written-order sweep of the current 54 bare-formal recipes.
catalog_path = ROOT / 'spec/ref-0.9.json'
raw = catalog_path.read_bytes()
recipes = {r['kind']:r for r in json.loads(raw)['recipes']}
pin_ref = '5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197'
assert hashlib.sha256(b'isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n'+raw+b'\n').hexdigest() == pin_ref
body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used
assert len(body_interfaces) == 54
changed = [k for k,v in body_interfaces.items() if v != sorted(v)]
assert len(changed) == 31
assert body_interfaces['value.select'] == ['p','b','a','t']

# Execute the existing generator only through document construction, before its file-write branch.
# The one-line correction is in memory; the published generator/fixture are never written.
generator = ROOT / 'conformance/fixtures/accepted-k82-09/generate.py'
source = generator.read_text().split('target = HERE / "k82.json"',1)[0]
assert source.count('return sorted(used)') == 1
scope = {'__file__':str(generator), '__name__':'pass2_in_memory_generator'}
exec(compile(source.replace('return sorted(used)', 'return used'), str(generator), 'exec'), scope)
corrected = scope['doc']
original_path = generator.parent / 'k82.json'
original = json.loads(original_path.read_text())
assert corrected['interfaces'] == body_interfaces
assert sum(original['interfaces'][k] != corrected['interfaces'][k]
           for k in body_interfaces) == 31
changed_edges = sum(a['interface'] != b['interface']
                    for a,b in zip(original['edges'], corrected['edges']))
assert len(original['edges']) == len(corrected['edges']) == 20
assert changed_edges == 10
without_enumeration = copy.deepcopy(corrected)
without_enumeration['interfaces'] = original['interfaces']
for old, new in zip(original['edges'], without_enumeration['edges']):
    new['interface'] = old['interface']
assert without_enumeration == original
assert sum(len(e['instances']) for e in corrected['edges']) == 86
assert len(corrected['cases']) == 16
corrected_path = SCRATCH / 'k82-body-order.json'
corrected_path.write_bytes(jcs(corrected) + b'\n')
for path in (original_path, corrected_path):
    output = run([PROBE, path, catalog_path])
    assert 'edges: 86 match, 0 differ' in output
    assert sum(': PASS' in line for line in output.splitlines()) == 16, output
print('PASS: K82 31/54 ordering mismatches;', changed_edges,
      'edge interface arrays differ; all other fields identical; both probes 86/86 and 16/16')
print('K82 original fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(original_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('K82 corrected scratch fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(corrected_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('PASS: pass2 verification complete; proposed schemas remain unimplemented/unaccepted')

## Remaining acceptance work

Council must decide and publish the exact repairs above before consumers
invent fields. Implementation owners then add the proposed positive and
negative vectors to suites C/F/H and relevant checker/producer tests.
552 needs actual producer adoption; ojy needs the fixture-owner repair;
the two dialect dispositions belong to that contract's council. Every
bead stays open for those decisions/work. This notes-only pass is complete
when the listed commands and finite checks pass; it does not close the
underlying contract or implementation obligations.
# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

Read the [round-1 report](../../../../bennett/docs/council/pass2-v2v-2026-11-02/round-1/report.md)
and both `out-sol.json` and `out-codex.json` in that directory. The table
maps every blocking item to this revision; it claims a proposed resolution,
not a new council verdict.

| Item | Change and witness |
|---|---|
| sol V2V-1; codex V2V-1 — frames lack a device-root boundary | Append the §12.3 rule below. Witness: device pair's root key is []; flip contexts belong to pair's tree; native main needs no descriptor. Separate root B has its own []. |

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. Every supplied frame_contexts array is validated, including a single-context
array. Coverage includes predicated-off and default-covered realised calls;
frame selection never inspects the quantum predicate value. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A uniform descriptor must also have valid allocation order, scheme identities
and live ranges in every covered invocation. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Fat-object applicability

Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
Append the following proposed normative paragraph to §12.3:

```text
In a mixed fat object, frame and frame_contexts describe the linked
ISA regions in the certificate's bound device-root realisation. Each
device root has its own object/root bindings and complete context set;
[] denotes that device root, never the native executable entry. Native
host functions, process argv/envp and host-only libc need no frame
records. Shared linked closures retain separate root-relative contexts.
A host may reach the device through §10's device CC entry; the general
mixed-container launch shim remains the explicit boundary work described
in the shared scope statement. Native comparison binds the corresponding
materialised device region and callee closure under its recorded configuration.
```

The shared statement lists today's assumptions and the exact remaining
container, launch, budget, handle, tape and host-memory contract changes.
This note does not assume that the device object is the entire binary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Concrete context witness

With H_pair denoting the pair closure hash, the two context keys are
`[{symbol:H_pair,point:"entry:0",iteration:[],instance:[],call_index:0}]`
and the same key with point `entry:1`. Their complete frame descriptors
are `{slots:[{id:"mut:0",role:"mut",class:"A",width:1}],scratch:[],history:[],borrow:[],ancillae:[]}`
and the identical descriptor with class B. Replace H_pair by the bound hash
and sort by unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. These five
arrays are all required B5 arrays. An empty schedule entries array does
not omit either default-covered call or its frame. A separate device
root's [] is independent of pair's [].

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked by [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py). The originating note reports the sibling test's `Unsupported` expectation
as gap evidence. This revision executes no sibling test and claims no
completed consumer fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.

## Publication and validation boundary

This is a revision proposal, not council acceptance or a consumer implementation.
The default publication rule in spec/README.md requires a full version bump
for semantic changes. The recommended 0.9 K route requires explicit council
and maintainer authorization of its in-version compatibility rules; this note
does not grant it. All unchanged-hash claims assume unchanged header/source
and catalog envelopes. A full version publication recomputes affected symbol,
object, target, schedule, certificate and versioned ref-library identities and
consumer pins even when recipe bodies stay fixed. Published 0.9 bytes remain
available; older closed-schema readers require an upgrade for new fields.

The reproducible finite and document checks are in [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py):
`timeout 120 python3 -I -B /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2/check_rev2.py`.
Executed for this revision: exit 0, all eight PASS result lines.
These finite checks verify witnesses and revision structure; they implement
no proposed-format checker or host shim. No full repository gate or sibling gate is claimed: scripts/pre-push
uses git/worktree writes, forbidden by this runner, and its setup changes paths
outside this job. Underlying beads remain open for publication and consumers.
# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

 succeeded in 63ms:
# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## Release and route

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate-context repair**, coordinated with
[cwq](b-isa-spec-cwq.md) and [jw7](b-isa-spec-jw7.md). Keep v2v open until
the encoding and consumer tests are accepted. A 0.10 deferral must retain
this explicit blocker; it cannot restrict the admissible call graph.
# Council — pass2-v2v-2026-11-02 round 1

Document snapshot sha256 31ffa07d499a; rubric docs/council/rubric-isa.md. Members: sol, codex. Scores are advisory.

| C7 | 4 | 4 | 4.0 |  |
| C8 | 4 | 4 | 4.0 |  |

Overall mean: 4.5

Verdicts: **sol: revise**, **codex: revise**

Core verdicts: **sol: accept**, **codex: accept**

**Consensus: no**   **Core consensus: YES**

Blocking by scope: sol: core 0 / contract 1 (blocking 1), codex: core 0 / contract 1 (blocking 1)

## Blocking issues

- **[sol] V2V-1** (Recommended exact spec diff, §12.3; Hashes, catalog and consumers; contract): The proposal changes certificate layout and frame inventory but never states how those records apply within the mixed fat object required by North Star §§2, 3.3 and 4.4. INDEX's general assertion that no capability is removed does not identify the device-root boundary, distinguish it from the native executable entry, or delimit the certificate inventory. This fails the council's explicit bennett-4ht standing check, which requires revise for silence on this boundary. The context mechanism itself otherwise preserves the required capability. *Fix:* Append to §12.3: 'In a mixed fat object containing native host code and marked device ISA modules, frame and frame_contexts coverage is scoped to the certificate's bound scheduled ISA root and its complete linked device-region invocation tree. The native host invokes device computation through §10's device CC entry and launch shim. The context key [] identifies the bound scheduled device-region root, not the native executable entry. Native host functions, process argv/envp and host libc are outside this frame inventory; marking a region does not require lowering the whole native executable. Native comparison uses the corresponding materialised region and its materialised callee closure under the recorded comparison configuration.' This permits the pair/flip device root to use [] while the enclosing native main has no frame-context entry.
- **[codex] V2V-1** (Recommended exact spec diff (§12.3); mixed-fat-object compatibility; contract): This changes certificate layout but supplies no mixed-fat-object integration statement. Neither the diff nor INDEX specifies that context inventories are scoped to device roots rather than the native host binary. This fails the explicit standing C7 check, which requires that statement for certificate-layout changes. Preserving nested region calls alone does not discharge it. *Fix:* Append to §12.3: 'For a mixed fat object, these frame records describe only the linked ISA regions in a bound device-root realisation. Each scheduled device root has its own certificate bindings and context set; [] is relative to that root. The native host component invokes device code through §10, using a device CC entry where required. Native host functions need no frame records, and this inventory does not require unmarked host code to be lowered to ISA. Certificates for different roots may reference the same linked closure, but their frame contexts remain scoped to their respective root bindings.' Alternatively place this rule in a shared normative mixed-object clause and explicitly incorporate it here; prefer the local paragraph.

## sol

The frame defect is real and the proposed context-indexed repair is technically sound. Basis results, resource arithmetic and catalog claims check out. One contract blocker remains: explicitly preserve the mixed native/device fat-object boundary.

**Path to accept:** Add V2V-1's mixed-fat-object applicability paragraph to the proposed §12.3 diff. No other blocking change is required; the context-selection mechanism, worked values and conditional hash claims are sound.

- **C1 5**: The diff changes certificate frame selection without changing §3 machine state or the step relation. *Change:* §12.3, optional clarification: 'Frame-context records introduce no additional machine state.'
- **C2 5**: The pair/flip source uses valid §4.7 action calls. Existing source points distinguish the calls; no instruction or semantic call edge changes. *Change:* §12.3, optional clarification: 'Selecting a frame descriptor introduces no instruction, semantic call or generated leaf.'
- **C3 5**: Selection uses resolved invocation data. Caller-owned controls remain separate, and distinct dynamic identities plus existing disjointness obligations prevent identical descriptors from implicitly authorizing storage sharing. *Change:* §12.3, optional clarification: 'Frame selection and validation do not depend on whether a quantum predicate is true on a particular basis input.'
- **C4 5**: §9.4 requires concrete classes and budget/parameter instantiation before forming c-only widths. The proposed selector handles both differences correctly. Independently checked pair costs are time=2, occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2 and peak[A]=peak[B]=1 under the stated sequential unit-duration schedule. *Change:* §12.3, optional clarification: 'A uniform frame must also have valid allocation ordering, scheme identities and live_ranges in every covered context.'
- **C5 4**: The defect exists: §12.3 has one closure-keyed record with one required B5 frame. Exact context coverage, canonical ordering and uniform shorthand repair it. The remaining blocker is the missing explicit mixed-fat-object boundary. *Change:* §12.3: add the mixed-fat-object applicability paragraph specified in V2V-1.
- **C6 4**: All four basis outputs and inverse restoration check out. The sibling test actually expects Unsupported, as disclosed. INDEX's arithmetic assertions do not exercise the proposed certificate schema; the note accurately limits its verification claim. *Change:* §15 suites F/H: 'The pair/flip fixture accepts entry:0 with class A and entry:1 with class B; omitted, duplicate, foreign, reordered or class-swapped frame-context records reject.' Publish the complete serialized fixture with those expected verdicts.
- **C7 4**: The repair preserves nested calls and target-dependent placement, supporting North Star §§4.2, 4.4 and 5.1. No false cited premise or misnumbered clause was found. Neither the note nor INDEX explicitly addresses the mandatory bennett-4ht mixed-fat-object check. *Change:* §12.3: state explicitly that frame coverage concerns marked device ISA regions and their linked closures within a mixed native/device fat object, as specified in V2V-1.
- **C8 4**: The encoding is compact and implementable using existing context identities. Under INDEX's authorized in-version route, §9.2 confirms that certificate changes leave source envelopes and object_hash unchanged. The 219-recipe catalog check reproduced envelope hash 5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197. *Change:* Hashes, catalog and consumers: 'Unchanged-hash claims assume the authorized in-version publication route described in INDEX; changing an object-header version changes object_hash, and changing the ISA envelope version requires recomputing affected symbol hashes and consumer pins.'

Suggestions:
1. §12.3: add 'Every supplied frame_contexts array is validated even when the region has only one invocation; it then contains exactly one matching record.' For a root-only fixture that record has context_key:[]. The existing exactly-one-of rule still prohibits simultaneously supplying frame.
2. §15 suites F/H: add 'Complete invocation-tree coverage includes predicated-off calls and calls covered entirely by schedule defaults.' In a fixture containing two statically realised flip calls, disabling one quantum predicate still requires both context descriptors; predicate values do not remove frame requirements.
3. Release and route: add 'Publish the certificate encoding decision before migrating consumers; consumers supporting this repair accept truthful legacy uniform frames and the new context-indexed form. Legacy consumers require an update before verifying certificates that need context-indexed frames.' Retain INDEX's explicit publication-route caveat.

## codex

The defect, cited premises, witness arithmetic and conditional hash claims check out. The context-indexed repair is sound. One contract blocker remains: explicitly preserve mixed-fat-object packaging and scope frame inventories to device roots.

**Path to accept:** Add V2V-1's normative device-root/mixed-fat-object scoping paragraph. No other blocking change is required; retain the proposed frame selection, complete context coverage and uniform shorthand.

- **C1 5**: The diff selects certificate metadata using the existing §12.1 invocation tree. It changes neither machine state nor the step relation. *Change:* Optional §12.3 clarification: 'Frame-context selection changes certificate interpretation only; the machine state and step relation remain unchanged.'
- **C2 5**: No instruction or call edge changes. §11/K63 already distinguishes the pair witness's two source call points, including their instance and call_index components. *Change:* Retain the consumer rule: 'Context keys identify existing invocation edges; this repair introduces no new instruction or call edge.'
- **C3 5**: The proposed descriptor selection preserves §9.4 ownership, dynamic allocation identity and live-range obligations. It neither shares storage implicitly nor changes coherent execution or cleanup. *Change:* Retain §12.3's proposed sentence: 'Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate from callee slots.'
- **C4 5**: The defect is real: §9.4 requires resolved classes and c-only width expressions, while §12.3 provides one frame per closure. The A/B witness is legal under §§8.3 and 11. Its sequential time, occupancy and peak values are correct. *Change:* Conformance vectors: state the exact resource keys: 'time=2, qubit_second[A]=qubit_second[B]=2, and peak_qubits[A]=peak_qubits[B]=1.'
- **C5 4**: Exactly-one representation, complete context coverage, canonical ordering and per-context validation repair the frame ambiguity. INDEX correctly aligns context identities across reports. The required mixed-fat-object certificate scope is unstated. *Change:* §12.3: 'In a mixed fat object, frame contexts belong to each bound device-root certificate; native host functions are outside this frame inventory.'
- **C6 4**: The checker obligation and negative vectors are derivable. Using the prebuilt reference interpreter, I verified all four pair outputs and four adjoint restorations. The INDEX frame/resource arithmetic passes; neither exercise implements the proposed certificate schema. *Change:* Conformance vectors/§15 F,H: 'The pair call at entry:0 selects class A and entry:1 selects class B, both width 1; swapping their classes rejects even though the program outputs remain correct.'
- **C7 4**: Repeated mixed-class calls remain admissible, consistent with North Star §§4.2 and 4.4. No cited clause is misquoted or misnumbered. However, neither v2v nor INDEX explicitly explains how the certificate change supports the required mixed native-host/device object. *Change:* §12.3: 'A native host binary may package these certificates with only its marked modules lowered to ISA, using the existing §10 device CC launch path.'
- **C8 4**: The representation is implementable using existing invocation identities and B5 descriptors. Under the authorized in-version route, §9.2 excludes certificate metadata from object_hash and symbol envelopes. No catalog bytes change; the published 219-recipe envelope hash independently reproduces. *Change:* Release and route: 'Legacy uniform frames remain valid after validation against every covered context; nonuniform legacy certificates must be regenerated, and consumers must support frame_contexts before claiming this repair.'

Suggestions:
1. Conformance vectors: publish a serialized pair/flip frame_contexts example. With H_pair denoting the pair closure hash, use keys [{symbol:H_pair,point:"entry:0",iteration:[],instance:[],call_index:0}] and [{symbol:H_pair,point:"entry:1",iteration:[],instance:[],call_index:0}], selecting mut:0/class A/width 1 and mut:0/class B/width 1 respectively. Include all five required B5 arrays. State: 'A default-covered call requires its record even when entries is empty; replacing the second class by A rejects.'
2. Release and route: record the requested compatibility decision explicitly: 'This is a council-authorized 0.9 K certificate repair with unchanged source/version envelopes. Existing uniform frame records retain their encoding and undergo all-context validation. Producers regenerate nonuniform certificates; consumers must not discard frame_contexts or substitute its first descriptor for the complete array.' This makes migration concrete without changing the ref@0.9 catalog.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

```
