# Checker question 4 — resources for repeated invocation contexts

Proposal for council review; source request 4 in
`../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA §12.1 enumerates every reached invocation context, including default
coverage; §§11/12.3 allow the same region under different controls,
arguments, budgets and schedule choices. Error entries are common bounds
keyed by variant/control count with explicit multiplicity composition.
But §12.3 region.resources and gates_used are flat maps. §8.2 defines
accounting for an event list, without choosing which invocation's list
these maps describe or declaring a max/sum/common bound interpretation.
K12's estimate must bind the reported resources, so it cannot resolve
this choice either.

This is an S0 contract gap for repeated contexts under rubric C4/C5.
North Star §§4.3–4.4/5.1 requires the full resource vector and
substitutable scheduler/checker interfaces. Ignoring repeated contexts,
rejecting controlled calls or silently taking one context would narrow
the promised machine. An explicitly unchecked flat report is honest
until the contract is repaired, but not a verified resource claim.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Take `@flip(t)` with body `x t`; root `@a(t,c)` executes
`action_call @flip (t) () () ()`, then
`ctl c action_call @flip (t) () () ()`. On a target whose native X
supports total control counts 0..1, duration 1, unit occupancy rate,
the two flip contexts have:

| Context | X count | Time | Qubit-time | Peak |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| forward, 0 inherited controls | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| forward, 1 inherited control | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 |

The inherited control is live even when its basis value is zero; these
are schedule resource charges, not counts of classically enabled gates.
For the root with both interface wires held throughout two sequential
unit slots, makespan=2, occupancy=4, peak=2 and two X-family native
events. Summing child peaks gives 3, not the root's peak; adding the
root plus its children would count the same leaves twice. These values
are hand-worked under the stated target, not new checker execution.

Even a key of (variant, inherited_controls) is insufficient in general:
different resolved token budgets in nested oracle calls create different
expansion sizes under the same key. Use the full §12.1 context identity.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Sum every invocation into the region's map | Counts aggregate work but does not define peak/time or let a checker replay individual invocations. |
| Common maximum bound per resource | Could be a separate upper-bound report; is not exact §8.2 accounting and needs a different assertion kind. |
| Pick one context implicitly | Depends on traversal/implementation choice; not reproducible. |
| Exact keyed contexts (recommended) | Reuse §12.1 identity and replay each invocation's event list; root totals remain independently defined. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Add to §12.3 region records:

```text
resource_contexts?:[{context_key,resources,gates_used,estimate?}]
```

Append after the schema:

```text
For a region reached in exactly one invocation context, resources and
gates_used report that invocation's exact §8.2 scheduled event accounting.
For more than one invocation context, resource_contexts MUST contain
exactly one record for every §12.1 context, in that section's canonical
enumeration order. context_key identifies the actual invocation, not
merely its variant or control count. The checker reconstructs its
arguments, budget, controls, placements, cleanup choices, schedule/default
decisions and native leaves, and recomputes every declared resource and
gate count. An estimate in that record uses §10.1 encodings and binds
exactly that context's values.

For compatibility the unkeyed resources/gates_used/estimate fields remain
the projection of the first canonical context; they MUST equal it and
discharge no other context's obligation. Missing, duplicate, reordered,
foreign or mismatched context records reject the report. A standalone
region with one explicitly bound invocation uses the single-context rule.

A root report counts its realised event list once, including semantic
callee leaves. Child context reports describe their own invocation
subtrees under §8.2 attribution; they are independently checkable views,
not extra costs to add to the root. This rule introduces no second
aggregation by summing child peak/time maps. Error-bound multiplicities
remain governed by their existing separate composition rule.
```

The request's alternative of leaving unkeyed maps semantically unused
would also avoid false verification, but still leaves meaningless data
required. The deterministic first-context projection above pins their
remaining role. Context records are exact reports, not a new optimizer
objective or a claim that every context has identical costs.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Certificate bytes and any report digest change. Object closure hashes,
target bytes, schedule bytes and ref catalog need not change. The root
schedule already determines contexts; the schema makes that binding
visible in resource reports. Scheduler, estimator, plugin and checker
must preserve full keys, canonical order and class-qualified K12 keys.
An old multi-context flat report needs recomputation/migration, not an
arbitrary max or sum guessed by the checker.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use both flip contexts and the root totals above; require control-count
0 and 1 to differ in occupancy/peak. Add two default-covered invocations,
repeated call occurrences, an lproc fixed instance, and differing nested
token budgets under the same variant/control key. Swap context records,
omit one, duplicate one, relabel its call_index, or mismatch its estimate:
reject. A single-context certificate keeps the old map form. The scalar
accounting witness is checked by
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md); no new
scheduler/checker test was run in this job.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate context repair** alongside the fault-rate
binding repair, without changing their distinct meanings. The existing
cross-repo filing request should carry this exact encoding decision.
After council acceptance publish schema and repeated-context fixtures,
then migrate producers and checker independently. This note alone does
not close that obligation.
# Checker question 3 — bind fault recounts to an invocation

Proposal for council review; source request 3 in
`../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA §12.3 `verification.fault_rate` contains domain, faults_by_code and
excluded counts, but no invocation. K13 gives each standalone claim its
own args/budget and a scheduled certificate its root invocation. Several
claims in the same region record can legitimately bind different args
or budgets. Neither §12.3 nor §12.1 says which one a flat fault_rate uses.
§5.2/§14.1 makes fault outcomes depend on the resolved token and args.
Thus a recount cannot be unambiguously replayed for a standalone region,
or for several reached scheduled invocation contexts.

This is an S0 contract gap under C5: counts for different invocations can
both be correct, and the schema does not select one. North Star §5.1
requires reported fault rates and §8 requires zero on acceptance domains.
Do not drop the rate, presume a convenient budget, or label an unchecked
count verified. These remain obligations for the complete demo ladder.

## Alternatives and worked witness

A memoryless oracle with x:wire[2], target:wire[2], fault:wire[4],
classical a:i2 has body `r = lift.add x a; lift.retval r`.
For args=["1"] and budget ["1","0","0","0"], every input reaches
the step limit before retval: 256 full interface tuples give
`faults_by_code:{"1":"256"}`. With the same args and budget
["2","0","0","0"], all 256 succeed, so faults_by_code is empty.
The input/output target and fault words vary over all values. A nonzero
incoming fault word is not an execution fault: recount code(F_B), not
whether the final XOR-accumulated fault word happens to be nonzero.

If the region carries claims for both budgets, a flat `fault_rate` can
mean either result. Neither the first claim nor the root invocation of
an unrelated caller may be silently selected.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Inherit from an arbitrary claim | Ambiguous; changes when claims are reordered. |
| Restrict every region to one claim/invocation | Removes existing certificate expressiveness. |
| Bind each rate explicitly (recommended) | Recounts exactly one invocation/domain, with context keys where scheduled. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Extend §12.3's existing standalone record to:

```text
fault_rate:{invocation:{args,budget},domain,faults_by_code,
            undefined_excluded,layout_dependent_excluded}
fault_rate_contexts?:[{context_key,invocation:{args,budget},domain,
                      faults_by_code,undefined_excluded,
                      layout_dependent_excluded}]
```

Append:

```text
Every fault_rate recount binds its own invocation by K13's exact typed
args, budget, validation and encoding rules. It inherits no claim's
invocation. Counts are recomputed from F_B on this invocation and the
declared domain, with the existing independently validated exclusion
classes reported separately; a nonzero incoming fault-register word is
not a fault event. Counts are nonnegative exact integers and cannot
exceed the enumerated applicable domain count.

For a scheduled region reached in several §12.1 contexts, report exactly
one fault_rate_contexts record for each such context, in canonical context
enumeration order. Each explicit invocation MUST equal that context's
resolved values. The legacy fault_rate record is the first context's
record with context_key removed; it is a deterministic projection and
discharges only that context. With one context the legacy record suffices
and MUST bind that context. Standalone records have their explicit
invocation and no implied context. Missing, duplicated or conflicting
bindings reject the requested recount; old unbound rate data cannot
discharge a verified fault-rate obligation.
```

A future arrays-only schema may remove the projection. This proposal
retains the required old field while making its meaning deterministic.
It does not change oracle fault semantics, exclusion classification or
K13's distinction between token tuple and process-local id.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Certificates gain invocation/context bytes and change identity; the
object/region closure, target, schedule and ref catalog do not change.
Plugin and checker must bind the same table entries/actual token tuples.
Rung gates must select the exact invocation for their zero-fault check.
No resource or fault result for one budget covers a different budget by
guessing; monotonic success can be a separate proved statement.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use both 256-tuple budgets above, reordered claims, args=[], tokens with
equal tuples/different ids, missing budget, conflicting scheduled context,
and duplicate/omitted context records. Check all fault values, including
an incoming word 1: budget-1 failure XORs it to 0 but is still code 1;
budget-2 success leaves it 1 but is not a fault. Counts for undefined
and layout-dependent exclusions must retain their own justified counts.
Scalar counts/XOR examples are checked by
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). The full
reference recount is a proposed acceptance test, not executed here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate binding repair**. The orchestrator's
existing cross-repo filing request should point at this exact change;
leave the obligation open until council accepts the encoding and checker
and producer owners implement the same standalone/context rules.

27:{"_type":"issue","id":"b-isa-spec-v2v","title":"§12.3 per-context frame descriptors (frame_contexts) for mixed-class realised contexts (from bennett-sched)","description":"Source: bennett-sched/.tmp/cross-repo-beads.md, \"Upstream request: per-context frame descriptors\". Local owner bennett-sched.\n\nDetermination: a legal 0.9 root pair has two wire[1] mut slots on classes A and B (capacity one each) and calls the same linked one-wire flip closure on each. The callee has two realised contexts with equal closure/variant/control-count keys and different resolved mut:0 classes. §12.3 has one region record per closure hash and one required §9.4 frame; each slot record has one concrete class and no context selector. Neither a class wildcard nor duplicate mut:0 slots is admitted, so one descriptor cannot describe both invocations. This blocks a complete certificate for a valid mixed-class request, independently of bennett-sched-enz.\n\nEvidence: bennett-sched tests/pipeline.rs::context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported.\n\nProposed exact normative text replacing the required frame entry in §12.3: \"Each region record MUST contain exactly one of frame or frame_contexts. frame is the §9.4 descriptor shared by every realised invocation context of that region. frame_contexts is an array of {context: \u003cfully resolved §12.1 invocation context key\u003e, frame: \u003c§9.4 descriptor\u003e}, in increasing bytewise order of JCS context encodings. It MUST contain exactly one record for every realised context, with no duplicates or omissions. The checker selects that context record before instantiating c and checks its classes, widths, lifetimes and interfaces against that realised invocation. A uniform descriptor MUST NOT replace descriptors that differ by classes or resolved parameter/budget-dependent widths.\" Add frame_contexts?: [...] to the §12.3 region schema, make frame conditional on the exactly-one rule, and add the two-class flip witness to suite F/H. Existing uniform frame records retain their format.\n\nNote: bennett-sched will not invent a certificate field before this decision. Related: bennett-circuits-4fa (heterogeneous slots) is a different gap (one slot across classes), not this one.\n","status":"open","priority":1,"issue_type":"bug","owner":"tobias.osborne@itp.uni-hannover.de","created_at":"2026-11-02T05:41:07Z","created_by":"tobiasosborne","updated_at":"2026-11-02T05:41:07Z","labels":["contract","defect","isa"],"dependency_count":0,"dependent_count":0,"comment_count":0}

# Bennett ISA reference interpreter

Rust 2021, standard library only. ISA 0.8 remains supported; ISA 0.9 migration
is in progress. Complete ISA 0.9 execution is not yet claimed. [PROGRESS.md](PROGRESS.md) records
what is verified, what is staged and in what order; [SPEC-QUESTIONS.md](SPEC-QUESTIONS.md)
records every reading the specification leaves open;
[council/response-round-1.md](council/response-round-1.md) answers the council's
round-1 review blocker by blocker. The crate is not yet a complete conformance
reference: the launch ABI (§10), target descriptions (§8), the schedule
consumer (§11) and the rigorous interval certificate checker (§12.1) are staged
as deliverables 8–11, and claims that need them are reported as unverified,
never as verified.

Build and test offline from the repository root (headless: `DISPLAY`,
`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `BROWSER` unset;
`TMPDIR=$PWD/reference-interpreter/.tmp`, created first):

```sh
cargo build --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline -- --ignored
```

The executable is `reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref`. Data
commands write one JSON object to stdout and exit 0; `help`/`--help` prints
plain usage. Diagnostics go to stderr with exit status 1, and ISA diagnostics
cite their rule. Integers above 2^53 − 1 are decimal strings (§9.1).

> **Council status (2026-10-31):** accepted by the sol and codex seats in round 24 of run `isa-ref-2026-10-29` on snapshot ad84e75 (461 tests, five exhaustive sweeps; rounds 1–24 under `bennett/docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/`). Staged deliverables 8–11 remain disclosed as staged. Muse seat held.

## Typed R faults and contract migration (ISA-7)

ISA 0.9 already resolves b-isa-spec-221: §0.2 K5, §4.1 `fault`,
§4.9 (implicit cause table, exact 64-bit terminal code, no successor or
later source effect, intact prefix history), §7.4, §10 (FAILED → REVERSED,
no result values, one fault record) and §14.3 T4. Zero maps to code 4;
nonzero codes, including program-defined codes, are preserved exactly.
This is a published 0.9 contract, not an amendment to accepted ISA 0.8.

The dialect 0.2.10 at `023f5a7` §11.5/§17.6 correctly reports
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` for every R entry targeting ISA 0.8: the mandatory
pre-instruction source step check needs `fault 1` before the instruction,
with the same effect-tape prefix and reversible history. `--budget` here
is host execution fuel, not that source counter; generic `trap` cannot
implement arbitrary typed R faults. Closing the ISA wording issue does
not clear that consumer blocker or certify rung 3. Open follow-up
`b-isa-spec-fuk` retains that producer/executor migration prerequisite.

The reference migration has landed explicit 0.8/0.9 envelope, ABI, identity,
linking and device-version dispatch, plus K5 typed terminal faults and eligible
FAILED-run reversal on 0.9. `tests/typed_faults.rs` checks source-instrumented
pre-instruction boundaries (zero and returns included), exact uint64 codes,
implicit causes, intact prefixes and entry restoration. The source counter
remains producer instrumentation; host fuel is a separate diagnostic.

Bound `ref` expansion, end-to-end K79–K81 execution, the 0.9 saved-tape matrix
and the remaining authoritative delta (including dynamic qregs, minted tokens,
parametrised qinplace and nonempty lproc forwarding) remain open under
`b-isa-spec-fuk.3`–`.7`. The producer and sibling contract pins have not been
migrated here. The full musl hello-world, native equality, R console, reset
and saved-tape replay acceptance remains owed (North Star §8 rung 3;
dialect §19.2). The council status above applies only to its pinned snapshot;
a new milestone acceptance round remains the orchestrator's responsibility.

## Commands

`check <object>` parses and validates the object, computes identities, and
verifies every stored interface hash. Output: `object_hash`, `status`
(`"ok"`, or `"unlinked"` when imports are unresolved), `symbols` (each with
`interface_hash` and `closure_hash`, null when its closure has an unpinned
import) and `unresolved_imports`.

`hash <object>` produces the same identities without comparing stored hashes
(for building fixtures; it edits nothing).

`run-oracle <object> <oracle> --input <x[,y...]> [options]` executes the §14.1
interpreter and prints `{"code":0,"payload":9,"steps":3}` for inc8(8) at B=3.
Inputs of any width are decimal or `0x` hexadecimal; `--input ""` is the empty
list. The oracle name may start with `@`.

Options:

- `--budget B` or `--budget B,heap,stack,depth`. Default B is 1,000,000.
  Layout limits supply heap and stack defaults; depth defaults to 64 with a
  layout; memoryless limits are zero.
- `--mem name=hex` supplies an interface region's bytes in cell order;
  unspecified regions start at zero; the byte count must be exact.
- `--arg value` supplies the next classical argument: an integer,
  `f64:0.5`, or `token:B,heap,stack,depth` (after `--budget` the abbreviated
  token takes its limits as defaults).

`step-check <object> <oracle> MODE [options]` runs the independent §14.1
interpreter and the literal §3.6 machine on each domain element, compares their
F_B, then runs the B inverse steps and compares every location, cell, counter,
stack cell, allocator entry and frame with the entry tuple (T2, T6). The domain
is every input bundle and every byte of every interface region not fixed by
`--mem`; target and fault entry values do not affect the computation (XOR
delivery). MODE is one of:

- `--exhaustive [--max-bits N]`: the whole domain, refusing domains above the
  user's guard N (default 16; no fixed ceiling);
- `--suite`: the §17 item 13 default, exhaustive at ≤ 16 bits and otherwise
  2^16 xoshiro256** samples;
- `--samples N`: N samples, N ≥ 1 (`--seed a,b,c,d` sets the four state words).

Output: `cases`, `input_and_memory_bits`, `mode` (`exhaustive` or `sampled`),
`fixed_regions`, `status`. Default B is 256; `--budget`, `--mem` and `--arg`
apply.

The four commands below share these conventions. A classical value is
`{"type","value"}`: `iN` and `ptr` values are unsigned decimal strings of
their bit-vector values (a signed reading at width N is the caller's), `f64`
is a JSON number, `token` is an object with `steps`, `heap`, `stack` and
`depth` (§9.1 integers), and `qreg` is the register id. Fault records are
`faults`, a list of `{"code","name"}` with the §5.4 code and name (`name` is
null outside the §5.4 table), plus `fault`, that code or null. Tape entries
are `{"args","bytes","dev","op"}`: the §7.3 tape triple plus the bytes the
operation transferred, hex. Measurements are `{"bits","reg","value"}` with
`bits` in wire order (wire 0 first). Draws select the first outcome, in
lexicographic order of the bits string (wire 0 first), whose cumulative Born
weight exceeds draw × total weight; integer values remain little-endian
(SPEC-QUESTIONS 29). Console output is `console_hex` always
and `console` when it is valid UTF-8, else null. `memory_digest` is the
SHA-256 of the complete §7.1 memory (allocation table, heap bump, every
nonzero byte).

For CC/R entries, arguments bind positionally to the entry signature (§10, `E_ARG` on a type
or count mismatch): `--args a,b`, `--input a,b`, or repeated `--arg value`,
at most one form (omission means no arguments). An `iN`/`ptr` value is decimal (negatives accepted within
the signed range) or `0x` hex, range-checked at the width; `f64:` prefixes a
binary64; `token:B[,heap,stack,depth]` is a §5.4-validated budget token
(the four-component `--budget` supplies its defaults, as for `run-oracle`);
`qreg:<bits>` allocates a fresh register and prepares those basis bits
(first character is wire 0). `--entropy hex` is the entropy tape. `--draws
d,...` queues measurement draws in [0, 1). `--replies file` is a JSON array
of `{"service","payload","reply"}` objects (payload/reply hex) consumed in
HOST_REQUEST order: each request must match the next entry's service and
payload, every request must find a reply, and every reply must be consumed,
else the run is a §10 diagnostic and no JSON is printed. `--mem global=hex`
replaces a layout global's image in the entry state, at exactly its size.
For CC/R entries `--budget B[,heap,stack,depth]`'s first component is
execution fuel (CC/R has no ISA step budget; exhaustion is a §3.4
diagnostic, default 1,000,000) and the rest are token defaults.

`run-cc <object> <entry> [options]` runs a CC or R entry on the
`ClassicalMachine` and prints the complete run. Output: `entry`, `status`
(`COMPLETED` or `FAILED`), `steps`, `budgets` (one per `token:` argument, as
for `run-program`), `results`, `fault`, `faults`, `tape`,
`console`, `console_hex`, `clock`, `effect_tape_length`, `memory_digest`,
`host_requests` (`{"address","capacity","payload","reply","service"}`),
`measurements`, and `trace`. Each trace record is one executed instruction:
`step` (counting from 0), `function`, `block`, `index` within the block,
`line` (the object text line), `op`, `lhs`, `operands`, `registers` (each
`{"activation","name","pre","post"}`, the changed dynamic instances with
their previous and new current values, null when absent) and `memory` (each
`{"address","pre","post"}`, every changed byte). A hostcall record includes
its reply's deltas. A fault or trap is a `FAILED` result with no values,
not a diagnostic. This includes runtime region-interface and control/target
aliases created through dynamic SSA: the call records fault 4 before changing
quantum state or consuming a handle. Controls may share read-only inputs.

`reverse <object> <symbol> [options]` runs forward, then reverses, and
reports whether the complete entry state is restored byte for byte:
`equals_entry` and `first_difference` (null when equal, else a description of the first
differing component). `status` is `REVERSED` when equal and `FAILED`
otherwise; a reversal the ISA forbids is a diagnostic citing its rule. The
form depends on the symbol kind. For a CC/R entry, `reverse` takes the
`run-cc` options plus `--steps N` (reverse only after N forward
instructions; without it the whole run); it is §7.4 whole-run or prefix
reversal of the retained history. It also reports `memory_equals_entry`
(the §7.1 memory component). Output: `entry`, the forward `results`, `fault`,
`faults`, `budgets`, `steps`, `tape` and `host_requests`, then the post-reversal
`console`, `console_hex`, `clock`, `effect_tape_length`, `memory_digest`
and `entry_memory_digest`. A faulted prefix reverses like any prefix
(§7.4); a run containing `measure` is a §10 `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE` diagnostic.
For an oracle, `reverse` takes the `run-oracle` options (`--input`,
`--budget`, `--mem`, `--arg`) plus `--steps k` (default B, above B is a
§5.4 diagnostic): it runs k forward steps of the literal §3.6 machine, then
k inverse steps (§14.1 prefix reversal, T2). Output: `oracle`, `budget`,
`payload` and `code` of F_k as read off the machine, `steps` (its executed

**Non-row addresses (K64).** Non-row support leaves have reserved
pseudo-points `$construct`, `$step`, `$commit`, `$release` in their
containing oracle; these are legal path points rather than user block
names. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P22]
For the bound `oracle.construct` recipe, its initial `visit State`
subtree has point `$construct`, each forward or inverse `step` invocation
has point `$step`, the commit segment from allocation of `halt` through
release of `halt` has point `$commit`, and the final inverse `visit State`
subtree has point `$release`. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29]
Within `step`, dispatch comparisons, HALT successor support, pc/next/frame
exchanges and dispatch cleanup stay at `$step`; `Rows[k]` and `Next[k]`
source-template subtrees instead retain source row k's point and shared
row ordinal space in traversal order, and semantic callees retain their
own invocation chains and spaces. [deps: ISA-3.6, ISA-11.1-P14, ISA-11.1-P22.S14, ISA-11.1-P29]
All nested helper `invoke`, loops and descriptor visits inherit their
containing point unless they enter one of those source-row or semantic
callee subtrees. [deps: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-11.1-P18, ISA-11.1-P22]
Number support leaves from zero in instantiated expansion order within
each pseudo-point, separately for each oracle step and phase at `$step`,
excluding all source-row and callee subtrees; inverse traversal retains
the corresponding forward step's ordinals and occurrence numbers. [deps: ISA-11.1-P29.S1, ISA-11.1-P29.S2, ISA-11.1-P29.S3, ISA-11.1-P9]
Reversing a complete already expanded construction retains each leaf's
forward pseudo-point and ordinal while adjointing its operation. [deps: ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P29.S3]
`$step` carries the containing oracle's t and fwd/rev phase; the other
three points have no step or phase at that level and match only wildcard
step/phase there, including when B=0. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P5.S8]
A pseudo-point's own instance and source-qloop iteration vectors are
`[]`; outer call edges retain their actual coordinates, and bound-library
constructor loops do not create source-qloop iterations. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P5]
Path validation accepts exactly these reserved spellings for oracle
support and validates their generated domains by the partition above;
B=0 has no `$step` leaves but still has construction, commit and release.
[deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-11-P7, ISA-11.1-P22]
These addresses annotate the bound event stream without removing,
reordering or changing the charge of any emitted leaf. [deps: ISA-11-P2, ISA-8.2, ISA-11.1-P29]

**Action-local region support (K83).** For each action or perm_action
invocation, collect all function-local qregion declarations, including
declarations nested in zero-trip qloops and in either qif arm. Each
declaration creates one region per invocation, not one per loop visit.
Traverse declarations in canonical function text order, recursively
visiting the then arm before the else arm and a loop body once, for
function-entry allocation and image preparation. Their terminal point is
$construct in that action. Function-exit image restoration and release
traverse the declarations in reverse order and have terminal point
$release. Within a declaration, use the bound library's region creation
expansion; restoration/release is its exact inverse. These support leaves
are unconditional as specified in §3.5 and §4.6.
Number leaves contiguously from zero within each of these pseudo-points
in forward instantiated support order, including allocation, image gates
and release leaves, excluding the action body and semantic callee
subtrees. Each leaf retains its allocation identity. Their own instance
and source-qloop iteration vectors are []; outer invocation edges retain
their actual coordinates. At that action level the support has no step
or phase and matches only wildcard step and phase, under §11 Matching.
A selector ending at an enclosing oracle level retains that level's
existing step/phase interpretation.
Raw inversion of an already expanded trace retains forward pseudo-points,
ordinals, invocation edges and allocation identities while adjointing
operations. Source-adjoint formation continues to follow K62.
Path validation admits $construct and $release for action and perm_action
support. $step and $commit remain oracle-only. A reserved point with no
leaves has an empty generated domain. K64's oracle partition and K65's
exclusion of implicit region cleanup are unchanged.
In a mixed fat object, the new support coordinates belong to action or
perm_action invocations within the linked device ISA object(s). Each
affected device object's schedules and certificates use them under the
existing object, closure and schedule bindings. Native host functions
require neither these support coordinates nor ISA region entries in that
device certificate. $construct and $release denote device action entry
and exit, not native process entry and exit; this repair does not
require the surrounding native binary to become an ISA object.

**Whole-cleanup selection (K65).** Each explicit computing-sequence/cleanup
pair has one non-emitting cleanup carrier at its source cleanup point,
identified before realisation expansion, even when the cleanup emits no
leaves. [deps: ISA-6.2-P1, ISA-6.4-P1, ISA-11-P3]
The point is the explicit `quncopy`, `qunload`, `lift.oracle_uncall` or
adjoint-call point when that instruction is the cleanup; for an explicitly
written inverse sequence ending in `qfree` or `qreturn`, it is that
terminating release point (also for an empty inverse sequence). [deps: ISA-4.3, ISA-4.5, ISA-4.6, ISA-4.2, ISA-5.3, ISA-5.5, ISA-6.2-P1]
The carrier uses that point's containing invocation chain, fixed-binding
instance, source-qloop iteration vector and containing step/phase, with
carrier ordinal 0; it creates no callee edge or leaf ordinal. [deps: ISA-11-P5, ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11.1-P29]
Only cleanup-kind entries match the carrier, with `generated: "*"` or
`generated: 0`; other generated integers do not match, and gate, alloc
and start entries do not match it. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P6.S5, ISA-11-P6.S6]
Resolve the unique maximal cleanup entry or the reverse default before
expanding that cleanup, and apply its reverse/recompute realisation to
the entire paired cleanup, subject to §6's validity conditions. [deps: ISA-11-P6.S1, ISA-11-P6.S7, ISA-11-P6.S8, ISA-6.2, ISA-6.4]
The carrier emits no gate, allocation, release, start event, resource
charge or timing dependency; ordinary gate/alloc/start selection then
applies to the chosen realisation's emitted leaves, and cleanup-kind
entries do not match those leaves. [deps: ISA-8.2, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11-P6.S6, ISA-6.4]
Internal constructor uncomputation, implicit qregion image restoration,
loop-flag cleanup and the certified private qinplace t/f cleanup keep
their instruction-defined expansions and do not add explicit-pair
carriers. [deps: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-4.6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-6.4]
For `d = qcopy s; quncopy s d`, selecting recompute at the quncopy point
chooses fresh-copy/XOR/uncompute for the whole cleanup, while reverse
chooses inverse-copy/release at the same carrier; an empty inverse
sequence still has its ordinal-0 carrier at its release point. [deps: ISA-4.5, ISA-6.2-P1, ISA-6.4-P1, ISA-11-P6]

**Source cleanup-pair partition (K81).** Consumers MUST form the following
partition from the source before K65 selection and K80 expansion. Match
source operation occurrences by §5.3 and §6.2, against the most recent
still-current computing occurrence on all of the operation's outputs;
matching restores its captured entry versions. A joint multi-output
operation and its inverse are indivisible. This is occurrence/version
matching, not equality of the composed unitary alone.

An intrinsic `quncopy`, `qunload` or `lift.oracle_uncall` pairs only with
its matching `qcopy`, `qload` or `lift.oracle_call`, respectively. Its
computing sequence does not absorb subsequent changes to the produced

## 0. Changes from 0.8

Every change from the accepted draft 0.8 has one entry below; nothing
changes without an entry. **Kind**: *E* = editorial (wording, or a
reading pinned that a conformant 0.8 implementation could already take,
with local semantics unchanged); *S* = semantic (some outcome or
well-formedness verdict differs from some reading of 0.8, or a
capability is added). **Hash**: every entry rides on the version bump V,
which changes envelope part (1) and every signature's `abi`, hence the
hash of **every** symbol (§9.2): the four 0.8 example hashes all change
and §16.3 republishes their objects (the 0.8 vectors remain valid 0.8
vectors); "+bytes" marks an entry that also changes the canonical bytes
of functions that use the affected form; "new" marks new symbols.
Sources: *EN* = an item of the evidence note
`docs/notes/2026-10-29-isa-09-evidence.md`; *bd* = a bead of this
repository; *SQ n* = entry n of `reference-interpreter/SPEC-QUESTIONS.md`
(its reading is adopted unless the entry says otherwise); *RC* = the
reference-interpreter council round 1
(`docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/round-1/report.md` in `bennett`),
member and finding; *r8* = a council round-8 suggestion (bead
`b-isa-spec-zsw.N`); *DL* = the dialect 0.2 outline's §25 list.

**No narrowing.** No entry rejects, faults, declares inert or removes a
capability that 0.8 permits or the north star promises. The entries
that add or sharpen a well-formedness check (E11's static view rule,
E16.9, E17.2, E17.3, E17.5's unmatched uncalls, E17.8, E17.9, E17.10,
E17.12, E17.14, the block terminators of K5) concern spellings that
always have a legal equivalent or uses that 0.8 did not define soundly
(E17.14: a location value as a classical argument would be superposed);
the run-time checks (E11, K1's width checks, K3's token validity, K4's
inverse-pair check) concern operations that 0.8 did not define or
already required (0.8's `qinplace` already demanded its certificates;
K4 admits library certificates as well as exhaustive claims, as the
north star §3.1 requires).

## 7. Memory and effects

### 7.1 Classical memory (CC, R)

Byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse. `layout` partitions the address
space: a **null region** `[0, null_size)` never allocated (null_size ≥
1); globals at fixed bases with images; the heap `[heap_base, heap_base +
heap_limit_max)`, all disjoint, `heap_base + heap_limit_max ≤ 2^ptr_
width`, and the number of globals ≤ 2^A_tab, all validated at load. `p =
alloc n` **fails before any mutation** when bump + max(n, 1) > heap_base
+ heap_limit_max or the table holds 2^A_tab entries (CC trap / R fault
`FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`); otherwise it returns the bump pointer, records
`(base, size = n, live = 1, pinned = 0)` and advances by `max(n, 1)`;
globals have pre-entered pinned live entries; an access is valid iff its
whole byte range lies in one live entry; `free p` marks the unique live
unpinned entry with base p dead (unpinned entries are heap entries,
whose bases strictly increase; pinned entries cannot be freed and a
pinned entry with base p is left unchanged; no live unpinned entry with
base p: CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT`); storage is never
reclaimed.
Initial state: bump = `heap_base`, table = the pre-entries in layout
order, memory zero except images.

### 7.2 Lifted memory of an oracle

`mem` has 2^H byte cells (H ≤ ptr_width); `alloctab` 2^A_tab entries
`(base, size, live, pinned)`; the stack 2^S_cells cells. The `layout`
fixes `null_size`, each global's base and image, `heap_base`, `heap_
limit_max`, and for each **interface region** of the oracle (W = 8
required) a base address; all ranges disjoint and within `[0, 2^H)`, and
for every oracle the number of pre-entries (globals plus its interface
regions) ≤ 2^A_tab, checked at load. `lift.load`/`lift.store` dispatch
by address range to `mem` or to the mapped interface region (role `in`
preserved by the construction). The low H bits address cells; `inrange`
guarantees the range lies in a live entry, hence within `[0, 2^H)`.

**Memoryless objects.** An object without a `layout` section MUST
contain no lprocs, no `lift.addr lift.boundscheck lift.load lift.store
lift.alloc lift.free lift.call lift.ret` rows, no `mem` interface slots
and no CC/R `alloc free load store`; H = A_tab = S_cells = 0, no `mem`,
`alloctab`, stack or counters are allocated, and `bennett_budget`
requires `heap_limit = stack_limit = depth_limit = 0` (§5.4). Example 1
of §16.3 is memoryless.

### 7.3 Device models and tapes (R)

The active effect tape is specified here; persistence and replay of saved execution journals are tool interfaces, whose interchange format and validation behavior are defined by an explicitly versioned replay profile.

Every device operation takes classical-memory operands and writes its
results to memory; each `effect` is **atomic**: validated (operands in
range, tape available) before any change, and a failing validation is a
fault record with no partial delta.

| Device | State (initial) | Operations | Failure | Frame records / reversal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `console` | byte sequence (empty) | `write(ptr, len)` | – | `len`; pop |
| `clock` | `c: u64` (0) | `read(ptr)`: writes c (8 bytes LE) at ptr, then c += 1 | – | overwritten 8 bytes; c −= 1 |
| `entropy` | position `p` (0) into the entropy tape (§10) | `read(ptr, len)`: copies tape[p..p+len) to ptr; p += len | p + len > length: fault `FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED` | overwritten bytes; p −= len |
| `block` | sector map from the `block_image` (else empty), 512-byte sectors; absent sectors read as zero | `read(sector, ptr)`; `write(sector, ptr)` | – | read: overwritten 512 bytes; write: the old sector contents **and a presence bit** (absent sectors are removed again on reversal) |

**BLOCK-IMAGE** (K10; source: bennett-vm-47i). `layout.block_image`
names a data symbol (§9.1, §16.1). Decode each successive pair of its
lowercase hex digits to one byte, in textual order. Its decoded length
L MUST be a multiple of 512, including L = 0. At block-device
initialisation, before executing any instruction or changing device
state, reject an image of any other length; never pad or truncate it.
For 0 ≤ s < L/512, sector number s is present and its byte at offset b
(0 ≤ b < 512) is image[512s + b]. There is no sector header, length
prefix, sector-number field or reversal of bytes within a sector. An
all-zero sector in the image is present. Every other sector is absent
and reads as 512 zero bytes; no image and an empty image give an empty
map. This specifies the initial map only; the §7.3 write/reversal
presence-bit rule remains in force. Motivation (0.8 §7.3): "sector map
from the `block_image` (else empty), 512-byte sectors; absent sectors
read as zero"; §9.1: "`data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`". The contiguous
encoding and partial-sector policy are additions, not a claimed 0.8
loader requirement.

**BLOCK-IMAGE witness**

```json
{"length":1024,"present_sectors":[0,1],"sector_first_bytes":[[0,0],[1,255]],"absent_sector":2,"partial_lengths_rejected":[1,511,513,1023]}
```

The image is 512 bytes 00 followed by 512 bytes ff (2048 hex digits).
Sector 0 is present although zero; sector 1 is ff throughout; sector 2
is absent and reads zero. Writing sector 2 then reversing removes it;
writing sector 0 then reversing restores a present zero sector.

Each `effect` appends `(dev, op, args)` to the effect tape.

### 7.4 Run history and the dynamic SSA model (CC, R)


 succeeded in 285ms:
fn action_state_json(run: &ActionRun) -> Result<Json> {
    let total = run.interface_wires.len();
    if run.state.wire_count() != total + run.borrowed.len() {
        return Err(fail(
            "§5.3: action left wires beyond its interface and borrow pool",
        ));
    }
    let mut entries = Vec::new();
    for (bits, (re, im)) in run.state.exact_support() {
        if bits[total..].iter().any(|b| *b) {
            return Err(fail(
                "§5.6: borrowed wires were not returned to their entry state",
            ));
        }
        let key: String = bits[..total]
            .iter()
            .map(|b| if *b { '1' } else { '0' })
            .collect();
        entries.push((key, Json::Array(vec![float_json(re)?, float_json(im)?])));
    }
    Ok(object(entries))
}
/// The definite basis value of one bundle, bit by bit, or None when some
/// wire is not in a basis state on every component.
fn bundle_value(state: &QuantumState, wires: &[u64]) -> Option<BitVector> {
    let mut value = BitVector::zero(wires.len()).ok()?;
    for (i, w) in wires.iter().enumerate() {
        value.set(i, state.read_basis(&[*w]).ok()? != 0).ok()?;
    }
    Some(value)
}
fn bundles_json(state: &QuantumState, bundles: &[Vec<u64>]) -> Json {
    Json::Array(
        bundles
            .iter()
            .map(|wires| {
                bundle_value(state, wires)
                    .as_ref()
                    .map(vector_number)
                    .unwrap_or(Json::Null)
            })
            .collect(),
fn reverse_action(program: &Object, name: &str, options: ActionOptions) -> Result<Json> {
    let f = program
        .functions
        .get(name)
        .filter(|f| matches!(f.kind.as_str(), "action" | "perm_action"))
        .ok_or_else(|| fail(format!("§5.3: unknown action @{name}")))?;
    let mut run = build_action_state(program, f, &options)?;
    let entry_state = run.state.clone();
    program.execute_action(name, &mut run.state, &run.bindings, options.adjoint, &[])?;
    let forward_state = action_state_json(&run)?;
    program.execute_action(name, &mut run.state, &run.bindings, !options.adjoint, &[])?;
    let state = action_state_json(&run)?;
    let equals = run.state == entry_state;
    Ok(object([
        ("action".into(), string(name)),
        ("adjoint".into(), Json::Bool(options.adjoint)),
        ("equals_entry".into(), Json::Bool(equals)),
        (
            "first_difference".into(),
            if equals {
                Json::Null
            } else {
                first_state_difference(&entry_state, &run.state, run.interface_wires.len())
                    .map(string)
                    .unwrap_or(Json::Null)
            },
        ),
        ("forward_state".into(), forward_state),
        ("in".into(), bundles_json(&run.state, &run.in_bundles)),
        ("mut".into(), bundles_json(&run.state, &run.mut_bundles)),
        ("state".into(), state),
        (
fn run_action(program: &Object, name: &str, options: ActionOptions) -> Result<Json> {
    let f = program
        .functions
        .get(name)
        .filter(|f| matches!(f.kind.as_str(), "action" | "perm_action"))
        .ok_or_else(|| fail(format!("§5.3: unknown action @{name}")))?;
    let mut run = build_action_state(program, f, &options)?;
    program.execute_action(name, &mut run.state, &run.bindings, options.adjoint, &[])?;
    let state = action_state_json(&run)?;
    let mem = object(run.regions.iter().map(|(region, cells)| {
        (
            region.clone(),
            Json::Array(
                cells
                    .iter()
                    .map(|cell| {
                        bundle_value(&run.state, cell)
                            .as_ref()
                            .map(vector_number)
                            .unwrap_or(Json::Null)
                    })
                    .collect(),
            ),
        )
    }));
    Ok(object([
        ("action".into(), string(name)),
        ("adjoint".into(), Json::Bool(options.adjoint)),
        ("in".into(), bundles_json(&run.state, &run.in_bundles)),
        ("mem".into(), mem),
        ("mut".into(), bundles_json(&run.state, &run.mut_bundles)),
        ("state".into(), state),
        ("wires".into(), number(run.interface_wires.len() as u128)),
    ]))
}
/// One-shot §10 launch: bennett_load (object parsed and hash-checked by the
/// caller), positional bennett_run of a device CC entry with copied
/// arguments and entropy, HOST_REQUEST/reply through the checked reply
/// queue, bennett_result_* reporting, optional bennett_reverse, and
/// bennett_run_close. Paths that need staged deliverables 8-11 (PROGRESS)
/// fail by name before any run is reported.
fn run_program(program: &Object, name: &str, options: CcOptions) -> Result<Json> {

fn run() -> Result<()> {
    let args: Vec<_> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();
    if args.is_empty() {
        return Err(fail(USAGE));
    }
    if matches!(args[0].as_str(), "--help" | "help") {
        println!("{USAGE}");
        return Ok(());
    }
    let command = args[0].as_str();
    if ![
        "check",
        "hash",
        "run-oracle",
        "step-check",
        "run-cc",
        "reverse",
        "run-action",
        "run-program",
    ]
    .contains(&command)
    {
        return Err(fail(format!("unknown subcommand {}\n{USAGE}", args[0])));
    }
    let path = args.get(1).ok_or_else(|| fail(USAGE))?;
    let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|e| fail(format!("{path}: {e}")))?;
    let program = Object::parse(&text)?;
    if matches!(command, "check" | "hash") {
        if args.len() != 2 {
            return Err(fail(USAGE));
        }
        if command == "check" {
            program.check_hashes()?;
        }
        let identity = program.identity()?;
        let symbols = object(identity.interface_hashes.into_iter().map(|(name, hash)| {
            let closure = identity.closure_hashes[&name]
                .clone()
                .map(string)
                .unwrap_or(Json::Null);
            (
                name,
                object([
                    ("closure_hash".into(), closure),
                    ("interface_hash".into(), string(hash)),
                ]),
            )
        }));
        // An object with unresolved imports is valid text but not executable
        // until linked (§9.3): say so instead of a bare "ok".
        let unresolved: Vec<Json> = program
            .section("imports")
            .map(|i| i.object().map(|o| o.keys().cloned().map(string).collect()))
            .transpose()?
            .unwrap_or_default();
        let status = if unresolved.is_empty() {
            "ok"
        } else {
            "unlinked"
        };
        println!(
            "{}",
            object([
                ("object_hash".into(), string(identity.object_hash)),
                ("status".into(), string(status)),
                ("symbols".into(), symbols),
                ("unresolved_imports".into(), Json::Array(unresolved)),
            ])
            .canonical()
        );
        return Ok(());
    }
    if matches!(command, "run-cc" | "reverse" | "run-action" | "run-program") {
        // Give CLI/IO/JSON input errors the relevant ISA context; preserve
        // more specific citations from the library. Print only a complete
        // result, so every error exits without partial JSON.
        let rule = match command {
            "run-action" => "§5.3",
            "reverse" => "§14.1 / §7.4 / §10",
            _ => "§10",
        };

            let name = args

                .ok_or_else(|| fail(USAGE))?
                .trim_start_matches('@');

Local owner bennett-sched.\n\nDetermination: a legal 0.9 root pair has two wire[1] mut slots on classes A and B (capacity one each) and calls the same linked one-wire flip closure on each. The callee has two realised contexts with equal closure/variant/control-count keys and different resolved mut:0 classes. \u00a712.3 has one region record per closure hash and one required \u00a79.4 frame; each slot record has one concrete class and no context selector. Neither a class wildcard nor duplicate mut:0 slots is admitted, so one descriptor cannot describe both invocations. This blocks a complete certificate for a valid mixed-class request, independently of bennett-sched-enz.\n\nEvidence: bennett-sched tests/pipeline.rs::context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported.\n\nProposed exact normative text replacing the required frame entry in \u00a712.3: \"Each region record MUST contain exactly one of frame or frame_contexts. frame is the \u00a79.4 descriptor shared by every realised invocation context of that region. frame_contexts is an array of {context: <fully resolved \u00a712.1 invocation context key>, frame: <\u00a79.4 descriptor>}, in increasing bytewise order of JCS context encodings. It MUST contain exactly one record for every realised context, with no duplicates or omissions. The checker selects that context record before instantiating c and checks its classes, widths, lifetimes and interfaces against that realised invocation. A uniform descriptor MUST NOT replace descriptors that differ by classes or resolved parameter/budget-dependent widths.\" Add frame_contexts?: [...] to the \u00a712.3 region schema, make frame conditional on the exactly-one rule, and add the two-class flip witness to suite F/H. Existing uniform frame records retain their format.\n\nNote: bennett-sched will not invent a certificate field before this decision. Related: bennett-circuits-4fa (heterogeneous slots) is a different gap (one slot across classes), not this one.\n"
